<a id='question-050'></a>

### Question 050: Humoral Immunity: Part 1 Focus 50 (constant)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the structure and function of immunoglobulins, specifically the constant region (Fc region) and its role in effector functions like complement activation and antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC), and how different isotypes (IgG subclasses) vary in these functions. (理解免疫球蛋白的结构和功能，特别是Fc区在效应功能中的作用，如补体激活和抗体依赖性细胞介导的细胞毒性(ADCC)，以及不同亚型(IgG亚类)在这些功能上的差异。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL) presents to the emergency department with fever, chills, and shortness of breath. He was recently treated with rituximab, an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody, for his CLL. Physical examination reveals bilateral crackles in the lungs. A chest X-ray shows diffuse bilateral infiltrates. Sputum Gram stain reveals numerous Gram-positive cocci in pairs. Sputum culture grows *Streptococcus pneumoniae*. Serological testing reveals markedly reduced levels of IgG1 and IgG3, while IgG2 and IgG4 levels are normal. The patient's C3 and C4 levels are normal. Flow cytometry of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a significant reduction in CD19+ B cells.

Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains this patient's increased susceptibility to *Streptococcus pneumoniae* infection?

(A) Impaired opsonization due to reduced IgG2 levels.
(B) Defective complement activation due to reduced C3 levels.
(C) Decreased antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) due to reduced IgG1 and IgG3 levels.
(D) Impaired alternative pathway complement activation due to reduced mannose-binding lectin (MBL).
(E) Reduced formation of immune complexes leading to decreased inflammation.

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 62-year-old male with CLL, treated with rituximab. This indicates immunosuppression due to both the underlying malignancy (CLL affects B cells) and the treatment (rituximab targets CD20 on B cells, leading to B cell depletion).
- **Presentation:** Fever, chills, shortness of breath, bilateral crackles, diffuse bilateral infiltrates on chest X-ray. This suggests pneumonia.
- **Microbiology:** Sputum Gram stain shows Gram-positive cocci in pairs (diplococci), and culture confirms *Streptococcus pneumoniae*, a common cause of community-acquired pneumonia.
- **Immunology:** Markedly reduced IgG1 and IgG3 levels, normal IgG2 and IgG4. Normal C3 and C4 levels. Reduced CD19+ B cells (consistent with rituximab treatment and CLL).
- **Question:** Mechanism explaining increased susceptibility to *S. pneumoniae*.

The key clues are the patient's immunosuppression (CLL + rituximab), the specific pathogen (*S. pneumoniae*), and the specific immunoglobulin deficiency (low IgG1 and IgG3). *S. pneumoniae* is an encapsulated bacterium. IgG antibodies, particularly IgG1 and IgG3, are crucial for opsonizing encapsulated bacteria, facilitating phagocytosis by neutrophils and macrophages. IgG1 and IgG3 are also the most potent activators of the classical complement pathway. While IgG2 is important for polysaccharide antigens (like those on *S. pneumoniae* capsule), IgG1 and IgG3 are generally considered more effective at opsonization and complement activation. The patient's low levels of IgG1 and IgG3 impair both opsonization and classical complement activation. ADCC, mediated primarily by IgG1 and IgG3 binding to FcγRIIIa (CD16) on NK cells and other cytotoxic cells, is also reduced. Option (C) directly addresses the reduction in IgG1 and IgG3 and its impact on ADCC, which, while less critical for *S. pneumoniae* than opsonization/complement, is a known effector function mediated by these isotypes. Option (A) is incorrect because IgG2 levels are normal. Option (B) is incorrect because C3 and C4 levels are normal, ruling out a classical or alternative pathway complement deficiency. Option (D) is incorrect because MBL deficiency is not suggested by the clinical picture or lab findings. Option (E) is incorrect; reduced antibody levels would likely *decrease* immune complex formation and inflammation, not increase susceptibility. The primary mechanism of defense against encapsulated bacteria like *S. pneumoniae* involves opsonization (mediated by IgG) and complement activation (also mediated by IgG). IgG1 and IgG3 are the main isotypes responsible for these functions. Their reduction impairs the host's ability to clear the infection. While opsonization is arguably the most critical function impaired here, ADCC is also a significant effector function of IgG1 and IgG3. Given the options, the reduction in IgG1 and IgG3 directly impacts ADCC.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Humoral Immunity & Immunoglobulins:** Antibodies (immunoglobulins) are produced by plasma cells (differentiated B cells) and play a central role in humoral immunity. There are five main isotypes: IgM, IgD, IgG, IgA, and IgE. IgG is the most abundant isotype in serum.
- **IgG Structure & Function:** IgG molecules consist of two heavy chains (γ) and two light chains (κ or λ). Each heavy chain has a variable region (VH, CH1) and four constant regions (CH2, CH3, CH4). The CH2 and CH3 domains form the Fc region, which mediates effector functions.
- **IgG Subclasses:** There are four subclasses of IgG in humans: IgG1, IgG2, IgG3, and IgG4, differing in their heavy chain constant regions, particularly the hinge region and the Fc region.
- **Fc Receptors:** The Fc region binds to Fc receptors (FcγRs) on various immune cells. Different FcγRs bind different IgG subclasses with varying affinities. For example, FcγRIIIa (CD16) on NK cells, neutrophils, and macrophages binds IgG1 and IgG3 strongly, mediating ADCC and phagocytosis. FcγRI binds IgG1, IgG2, and IgG3, mediating phagocytosis. FcγRIIa binds IgG1 and IgG2, mediating phagocytosis and ADCC.
- **Complement Activation:** IgG1, IgG2, and IgG3 can activate the classical complement pathway by binding to C1q. IgG3 is the most potent activator, followed by IgG1, then IgG2. IgG4 does not activate complement effectively. Complement activation leads to opsonization (C3b deposition), inflammation (C3a, C5a), and direct lysis (MAC formation).
- **Opsonization:** IgG antibodies coat pathogens, enhancing their phagocytosis by cells expressing FcγRs. This is particularly important for encapsulated bacteria like *S. pneumoniae*.
- **ADCC:** IgG antibodies bind to infected or tumor cells, and NK cells or other cytotoxic cells bind to the Fc region of the antibody via FcγRIIIa (CD16), leading to the release of cytotoxic granules and killing of the target cell.
- **Patient's Condition:** The patient has CLL and received rituximab, leading to B cell depletion and reduced antibody production. The specific deficiency in IgG1 and IgG3 impairs both opsonization and classical complement activation, as well as ADCC. *S. pneumoniae* is susceptible to opsonization and complement-mediated killing.
- **Kaplan/First Aid Reference:** Kaplan Preclinical Immunology and Microbiology Review 2023, pp. 74–86 details IgG subclasses and their functions. First Aid 2025 (Immunology: Humoral Immunity, Immunoglobulins & Isotypes) also covers IgG subclasses and effector functions.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Impaired opsonization due to reduced IgG2 levels. This is incorrect because the patient has *normal* IgG2 levels. While IgG2 is important for polysaccharide antigens like the *S. pneumoniae* capsule, the patient's deficiency is in IgG1 and IgG3, which are also crucial for opsonization and complement activation.
- (B): Defective complement activation due to reduced C3 levels. This is incorrect because the patient's C3 and C4 levels are *normal*. Complement activation is impaired due to the lack of IgG1 and IgG3, which are needed to initiate the classical pathway, but the complement components themselves are present.
- (D): Impaired alternative pathway complement activation due to reduced mannose-binding lectin (MBL). This is incorrect. MBL deficiency affects the lectin pathway of complement activation. There is no information suggesting MBL deficiency, and the patient's complement levels (C3, C4) are normal, indicating the classical and alternative pathways are intact in terms of component levels, although activation via the classical pathway is impaired due to lack of activating antibodies.
- (E): Reduced formation of immune complexes leading to decreased inflammation. This is incorrect. Reduced antibody levels (IgG1 and IgG3) would lead to *less* immune complex formation, which might *decrease* inflammation, not increase susceptibility to infection. Increased susceptibility is due to impaired clearance of the pathogen, not decreased inflammation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | IgG1/IgG3 Deficiency (Patient) | IgG2 Deficiency | Complement Deficiency (e.g., C3) | MBL Deficiency |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------|
| **Key Defect**       | Low IgG1, Low IgG3             | Low IgG2        | Low C3 (or other components)      | Low MBL         |
| **Primary Impact**   | Impaired opsonization, complement activation (classical), ADCC | Impaired opsonization of polysaccharide antigens | Impaired all complement pathways | Impaired lectin pathway complement activation |
| **Susceptibility**   | Encapsulated bacteria, viruses | Encapsulated bacteria (especially *S. pneumoniae*, *H. influenzae*) | Encapsulated bacteria (especially *Neisseria*) | Encapsulated bacteria |
| **Complement Levels**| Normal C3/C4 (activation impaired) | Normal C3/C4 (activation impaired) | Low C3/C4 (component deficiency) | Normal C3/C4 (lectin pathway impaired) |
| **Patient's Findings**| Low IgG1/G3, Normal IgG2/G4, Normal C3/C4 | N/A             | N/A                               | N/A             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary effector functions of IgG1 and IgG3 are opsonization, classical complement pathway activation, and ADCC. Deficiency in these isotypes, as seen in this patient due to CLL and rituximab treatment, increases susceptibility to infections, particularly by encapsulated bacteria like *S. pneumoniae*. Remember that different IgG subclasses have distinct roles, and specific deficiencies lead to characteristic susceptibilities. Differentiate this from deficiencies in other isotypes (e.g., IgG2 deficiency leading to susceptibility to polysaccharide-encapsulated organisms) or complement components.
